# R2202 Residential Appliance Saturation Survey (RASS) – Detailed Tables

**Source**: Meek - R2202 Residential Appliance Saturation Survey (RASS) – Detailed Tables.pdf
**Pages**: 183 total
**Date**: 9/17/2025 (printed on the cover, with the label "FINAL")
**Author(s)**: Eugene McGowan, Melissa Meek, & Kailey Pratt, NMR Group, Inc.
**Author affiliations**: NMR Group, Inc. No correspondence block or numbered affiliation list is printed in the PDF.

_Extractor note (not source text): Every table is transcribed as a markdown table in the PDF's own row and column order, one markdown table per printed table. A table that continues onto a following page is split at the page marker and the column header is repeated, as the PDF repeats it. Superscript significance letters in cells (for example `76%a`) are rendered as `76%<sup>a</sup>`, and the table notes that explain them are listed under each table as `Notes:`. Empty cells are empty in the PDF. Cells printed as `--` or `–` are kept as printed. The text layer printed a few column headers broken across two lines or cut mid-word (for example "Statewi" / "de" for "Statewide", "Non-" / "LMI"); these are joined silently. In the heating-system column headers the PDF prints "SF 2 to 4 Units" and "MF 5+ Units" over two header lines; they are rendered as single header cells. The Appendix B-F consumer-electronics and renewables tables carry no printed "(Source: ...)" line, and none is added. Table 44 is the only table with indented sub-rows (fuel under each water-heater type); parent rows are bold and sub-rows are indented with non-breaking spaces. The text layer dropped the leading "I" of "I’m not sure" in Table 342 (income) and it was restored from the rendered page. Source anomalies are kept as printed and flagged where they occur. Running headers ("R2202 RESIDENTIAL APPLIANCE SATURATION SURVEY (RASS) – DETAILED TABLES") and the printed page numbers are omitted; every `printed p.` value below equals the PDF page number and was confirmed against each page's footer. The cover photograph and the certification logos on page 1 are not transcribed._

## Introduction

This document contains detailed tables related to the Connecticut R2202 Residential Appliance Saturation Survey (RASS) study; key findings and recommendations can be found in the accompanying report. This detailed document contains tables displaying penetration, fuel type, efficiency, age, and other analyses for key measures included in the study such as HVAC, water heaters, and appliances. All data presented is weighted, and penetration and saturation data for heating and cooling systems used adjustment factors based on verified equipment to account for respondent self-reporting error. Statistical significance is indicated in tables with superscript letters throughout, if no specific indication is made then it should be assumed that the result is not statistically significant. Specific details on the methodology used to collect and analyze data can be found in the main report document.

The sections in this document are organized by key demographic variables of interest:

- Appendix A Results by Home Type
- Appendix B Results by Tenure
- Appendix C Results by Income
- Appendix D Results by Program Participation
- Appendix E Results by Utility
- Appendix F Results by EJ (Environmental Justice Community)

Table 1 shows universal confidence intervals that can be used for all data in tables presented throughout, where the source is survey or self-audit data (a majority of tables). Table 2 shows confidence intervals for data in tables based on the virtual audit sample only (very few tables). Table subcaptions provide the source of the data in each table. As an example, Table 3 lists 36% of homes primarily heating with fuel oil. Referring to Table 1 (since this table is based on survey or self-audit data as opposed to virtual audit data only), the confidence interval for proportions from 36-40% is 2.53%, therefore the approximate confidence interval for homes heating primarily with oil would be 36% +/- 2.53%, or (33.47%, 38.53%).

Below are definitions of terms that are used in the report and this appendix document:

- **Single-family** refers to single-family detached or standalone houses, including mobile homes, attached homes that share a wall with another home (e.g., townhomes), and small multifamily homes containing two to four units.
- **Multifamily** refers to homes in buildings with five or more units.
- **Low-income** includes respondents eligible for LIHEAP (60% of state median income) based on their reported household occupancy and 2023 income.
- **Moderate-income** includes respondents whose household incomes are greater than 60% of state median income (LIHEAP-eligible) but less than or equal to 80% state median income based on their household occupancy.

[PDF p. 4 | printed p. 4]

- **Non-low- or moderate-income (non-LMI)** includes respondents with household incomes greater than 80% of the state median income.
- **Environmental Justice (EJ) Community**[^1]: Defined by DEEP as either a distressed municipality as designated by the Connecticut Department of Economic and Community Development, or defined census block groups where 30% of the population is living below 200% of the federal poverty level.
- **Penetration** is the number of homes that have at least one instance of the relevant measure. Taking heating as an example, the penetration of boilers is the _percentage of homes_ that have at least one boiler.
- **Saturation** is the amount of a measure category that is represented by a specific technology or subtype. Using the same heating example, the saturation of boilers would be the _percentage of all heating systems_ that are boilers.

[^1]: [DEEP: Learn more about environmental justice communities](https://portal.ct.gov/deep/environmental-justice/05-learn-more-about-environmental-justice-communities)

[PDF p. 5 | printed p. 5]

## Appendix A Results by Home Type

The following sections contain detailed results by home type. The source of the data in each table is specified in the table subcaption:

- Tables that use survey, self-audit, and virtual audit data use verified data (nameplate photos or virtual audit documentation) when available and self-reported data otherwise.
- Tables that use self-audit and virtual audit data summarize data that can only reliably be captured through nameplate photos and documentation, such as efficiency, age of manufacture, and ENERGY STAR status.
- Tables that use virtual audit data only summarize data that was only collected through visual inspection at the virtual audit (e.g., presence of faucet aerators).
- Tables that use survey data only refer to summaries of survey questions that have no additional study documentation (e.g., awareness of Energize Connecticut programs).

## Appendix B Results by Tenure

The following section contains detailed results by tenure (homeowners and renters). The source of the data in each table is specified in the table subcaption:

- Tables that use survey, self-audit, and virtual audit data use verified data (nameplate photos or virtual audit documentation) when available and self-reported data otherwise.
- Tables that use self-audit and virtual audit data summarize data that can only reliably be captured through nameplate photos and documentation, such as efficiency, age of manufacture, and ENERGY STAR status.
- Tables that use virtual audit data only summarize data that was only collected through visual inspection at the virtual audit (e.g., presence of faucet aerators).
- Tables that use survey data only refer to summaries of survey questions that have no additional study documentation (e.g., awareness of Energize Connecticut programs).

## Appendix C Results by Income

This appendix presents detailed results by income categories.

Survey respondents indicated whether their 2023 household income was more or less than the LIHEAP eligibility threshold for their household size and also specified their income range in a follow-up question. Income categories are defined as follows:

- **Low-income** includes respondents eligible for LIHEAP or whose 2023 household incomes were $70,000 or less.
- **Moderate-income** includes respondents whose household incomes are too high to qualify for LIHEAP, but less than $100,000. If LIHEAP eligibility is unknown, this category includes respondents with a household income of $70,001 to $100,000, the definition of moderate-income for married householders filing taxes jointly.[^3]
- **Non-low- or moderate-income (non-LMI)** includes respondents with household incomes of $100,000 or more.
- The **refused** category includes respondents who declined to answer either income question or partial survey respondents who were not shown the income question.

The source of the data in each table is specified in the table subcaption:

- Tables that use survey, self-audit, and virtual audit data use verified data (nameplate photos or virtual audit documentation) when available and self-reported data otherwise.
- Tables that use self-audit and virtual audit data summarize data that can only reliably be captured through nameplate photos and documentation, such as efficiency, age of manufacture, and ENERGY STAR status.
- Tables that use virtual audit data only summarize data that was only collected through visual inspection at the virtual audit (e.g., presence of faucet aerators).
- Tables that use survey data only refer to summaries of survey questions that have no additional study documentation (e.g., awareness of Energize Connecticut programs).

[^3]: Tax filing status was not collected as part of this study and the married-filing-jointly income threshold was applied to all respondents. The survey asked respondents to indicate whether their income was less than $70,000, between $70,000 and $99,999, or $100,000 or more.

## Appendix D Results by Program Participation

The following section contains detailed results by self-reported program participation. In this case a ‘participant’ is defined as a respondent who indicated participation in any type of Energize CT program within the past two years. Participation in any program does not indicate that the specific measures detailed in any give table were incentivized by the program. For example, a respondent may have had an energy audit completed at their home and would therefore be categorized as a ‘participant’ in a table detailing boiler efficiency, but this does not mean that their boiler was replaced and incentivized by the program. The source of the data in each table is specified in the table subcaption:

- Tables that use survey, self-audit, and virtual audit data use verified data (nameplate photos or virtual audit documentation) when available and self-reported data otherwise.
- Tables that use self-audit and virtual audit data summarize data that can only reliably be captured through nameplate photos and documentation, such as efficiency, age of manufacture, and ENERGY STAR status.
- Tables that use virtual audit data only summarize data that was only collected through visual inspection at the virtual audit (e.g., presence of faucet aerators).
- Tables that use survey data only refer to summaries of survey questions that have no additional study documentation (e.g., awareness of Energize Connecticut programs).
